Let's design `project_feasible(h,n)` more robust:
```
target = n/2.0
h = np.clip(h,0,1)
for _ in range(100):
    total = h.sum()
    diff = target - total
    if abs(diff) < 1e-13: break
    if diff >0:
        weights = 1 - h
        s = weights.sum()
        if s < 1e-15: break
        h = h + diff/s*weights
    else:
        s = h.sum()
        if s <1e-15: break
        h = h + diff/s*h
h = np.clip(h,0,1)
# exact adjust
for _ in range(10):
    diff = target - h.sum()
    if abs(diff) < 1e-14: break
    if diff >0:
       idx = np.argmin(h) # most slack? maybe argmax(1-h)
       # but scaling may change; adjust with min(diff, 1-h[idx])
       adj = min(diff, 1-h[idx])
       h[idx] += adj
    else:
       idx = np.argmax(h)
       adj = min(-diff, h[idx])
       h[idx] -= adj
return h
```
If one variable insufficient, loop. But if many saturated? 100 loops enough? We can explicitly distribute over all with capacities:
```
if diff>0:
  w=1-h; total_w=w.sum(); if total_w>...: h += diff*w/total_w (proportional capacity)
```
This may push some to 1; repeat. At end, adjust one. Good.

Need map full index to sign/e:
```
def term_grad(h,n, full_idx, dx):
   n1=n-1
   g=np.zeros(n)
   if full_idx == n1:
       g = (1-2*h)*dx
   elif full_idx < n1:
       e = n1 - full_idx
       L = n-e

LP directions:
```
def trust_region_lp_step(h,n,deadline, top_count, r):
   vals = np.correlate(h,1-h)
   c = vals.max()*dx
   # active idx top top_count or vals >= c - tol perhaps
   order = np.argsort(vals)[::-1]
   active = order[:top_count]
   F = vals[active]
   G = np.vstack([term_grad(h,n,idx,dx) for idx in active])
   # LP variable [δ, t]
   m = len(active)
   A_ub = np.zeros((m,n+1))
   b_ub = np.zeros(m)
   for i in range(m):
       A_ub[i,:n]=G[i]
       A_ub[i,n]=-1
       b_ub[i]=-F[i]
   bounds = [(max(-h[i], -r), min(1-h[i], r)) for i in range(n)] + [(None,None)]
   res=linprog(c_obj, A_ub=A_ub,b_ub,bounds, A_eq=np.ones((1,n)),b_eq=0,method='highs')
   δ=res.x[:n]; δ-=δ.mean()
   # line search
```

Pseudo:
```
def trust_region_lp_step(h,n,deadline,top_count,trust):
    vals_counts = np.correlate(h, 1-h)
    c_counts = vals_counts.max()
    if not np.isfinite(c_counts): return None
    # active indices:
    # need full index order from np.correlate. Use np.argsort descending. To include near max more stable:
    order=np.argsort(vals_counts)[::-1]
    active=order[:top_count]
    # add indices with vals_counts > c_counts - max(1e-8, 1e-5*c_counts) maybe up to top_count*2? We'll combine:
    tol = max(1e-10, 1e-6)
    near = np.where(vals_counts >= c_counts - tol)[0]
    active = np.unique(np.concatenate([near, order[:top_count]]))
    if len(active)>400: active=order[:400]
    m=len(active)
    G=np.empty((m,n))
    for i,idx in enumerate(active): G[i]=term_grad(h,n,idx)  # counts scale
    # LP
    cobj=np.zeros(n+1); cobj[n]=1.0
    A=np.zeros((m,n+1)); b=np.zeros(m)
    for i,idx...:
       A[i,:n]=G[i]; A[i,n]=-1.0; b[i]=-vals_counts[idx]
    # bounds
    low = np.maximum(-h, -trust); high = np.minimum(1-h, trust)
    # if low>high clamp
    bounds = list(zip(low,high)) + [(None,None)]
    Aeq=np.ones((1,n)); beq=np.array([0.0])
    res=linprog(cobj,A_ub=A,b_ub=b,A_eq=Aeq,b_eq=beq,bounds=bounds,method='highs')
    if res.status not in (0,3)? HiGHS status 0 optimal; 1? 3? 3 infeasible? Actually scipy Highs: 0 optimal, 1? 'optimal'? But use res.success.
    if not res.success or res.x is None: return False,...
    delta=res.x[:n]
    # enforce sum exactly:
    delta -= delta.mean()
    # line search
    best_step=None
    for stol in [1.0,0.7,0.45,0.25,0.12,0.06,0.03]:
       h2=project_feasible(h + stol*delta, n)
       c2=compute_c5(h2,n)
       if c2 < c_counts - 1e-12? actually c in objective not counts; use c_obj2 < c_obj - 1e-12:
          return True,h2,c2,stol
    return False,h,c,0
```

Compute F (objective values) and softmax weights. To compute weighted gradient efficient:
```
def smooth_grad(h,n,alpha):
   F = np.correlate(h,1-h)*dx  # length M
   cmax=F.max()
   a=alpha*(F-cmax)
   # a max 0; subtract max for stability (already)
   exp=np.exp(a)
   w=exp/exp.sum()
   # skip tiny weights
   active_idx=np.where(w>1e-8)[0]
   g = sum(w_i * term_grad_counts(h,n,idx)*dx)
   phi = cmax + log(sumexp)/alpha
```

def smooth_obj_grad_penalty(x,n,alpha,pen):
    val, grad = smooth_phi_grad(x,n,alpha)
    s = x.sum() - n/2
    val += 0.5*pen*s*s
    grad += pen*s*np.ones(n)
    return val, grad
```

Detailed function designs:

1. `compute_c5(h,n)` exact:
```
def compute_c5(h,n):
    dx=2.0/n
    if n==1: ... but n even? For safety.
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    return float(np.max(np.correlate(h, 1.0-h, mode='full')) * dx)
```
But for large n, np.correlate may be slow; only use when needed.

2. `compute_values(h,n)` returns full counts (np.correlate). Use float.

3. `project_feasible(h,n)`: as above, ensure bounds/sum. Need avoid modifying input? make copy.

Potential problem with `project_feasible` using proportional weights can slightly alter values and make h non-binary. If we want binary, don't project; use to_binary. For LP candidates, project exactly.

`project_feasible` exact sum:
```
def project_feasible(h,n):
    target = n/2.0
    h = np.clip(np.array(h, dtype=float, copy=True),0,1)
    for _ in range(50):
        s = h.sum()
        diff = target - s
        if abs(diff) < 1e-13: break
        if diff > 0:
            w = 1.0 - h
            ws = w.sum()
            if ws < 1e-15: break
            h += (diff/ws)*w
        else:
            ws = h.sum()
            if ws < 1e-15: break
            h += (diff/ws)*h
    h = np.clip(h,0,1)
    # final fine adjust loop over all variables with capacity
    for _ in range(10 + n*2):
        s=h.sum(); diff=target-s
        if abs(diff)<1e-14: break
        if diff>0:
            idxs=np.argsort(h)  # most room
            # adjust sequentially
            for idx in idxs:
                if abs(diff)<1e-15: break
                add=min(diff, 1.0-h[idx])
                h[idx]+=add; diff-=add
        else:
            idxs=np.argsort(h)[::-1]
            for idx in idxs:
                if abs(-diff)<1e-15: break
                sub=min(-diff, h[idx])
                h[idx]-=sub; diff+=sub
    return h
```
For large n, argsort loops heavy if final diff not tiny; but after proportional likely tiny. Use if abs diff >1e-13 only. Fine.

4. `term_grad_counts(h,n,full_idx)`:
```
def term_grad_counts(h,n,full_idx):
    dx=1.0 # counts
    n1=n-1
    g=np.zeros(n,dtype=float)
    if full_idx == n1:
        return 1.0 - 2.0*h
    elif full_idx < n1:
        e = n1 - full_idx
        L = n - e
        # U_e: sum h_i (1 - h_{i+e})
        g[:L] += 1.0 - h[e:n]
        g[e:n] += -h[:L]
    else:
        e = full_idx - n1
        L = n - e
        # V_e
        g[e:n] += 1.0 - h[:L]
        g[:L] += -h[e:n]
    return g
```
For counts; objective gradient = scaled by dx if needed.
Check for e>n? e in 1..n-1; L positive. OK.

5. `smooth_phi_grad(x,n,alpha,penalty=0.0)` top-level for L-BFGS? We may implement but maybe not use. Let's implement lightweight for projected gradient.

```
def smooth_phi_grad_obj(x,n,alpha):
   vals = np.correlate(x,1-x) # counts
   obj = vals * (2.0/n)
   cmax = obj.max()
   a = alpha*(obj - cmax)
   # a max 0; exp stable
   expa = np.exp(a)
   sumexp = expa.sum()
   phi = cmax + np.log(sumexp)/alpha
   w = expa/sumexp
   active = np.where(w > 1e-9)[0]
   gd = np.zeros(n)
   dx=2.0/n
   for idx in active:
       gd += w[idx] * term_grad_counts(x,n,idx)
   gd *= dx
   return phi, gd
```
Note if alpha very large, exp underflow for nonmax -> OK.

6. `projected_smooth_polish(h,n,deadline, alphas)`:
```
def projected_smooth_polish(h,n,deadline, alphas):
    best=h.copy(); best_c=compute_c5
    for alpha in alphas:
       h=best.copy(); c=best_c
       for it in range(3000):
          if time.time()>deadline: break
          phi,g = smooth_phi_grad_obj(h,n,alpha)
          nrm = np.linalg.norm(g, ord=np.inf)
          if nrm < 1e-13: break
          d = -g/nrm
          accepted=False
          # try steps
          for s in [0.05,0.1,0.02,0.01,0.2,0.005]:
              h2=project_feasible(h + s*d, n)
              c2=compute_c5(h2,n)
              if c2 < c - 1e-13:
                  h,c = h2,c2; accepted=True; break
          if not accepted: break
          if c < best_c: best,c=...
    return best,best_c
```
Maybe gradient direction is -phi; if c doesn't decrease but phi does, maybe c later. To avoid rejecting good phi directions, accept if c2 < c - 1e-14 or phi2 < phi -1e-8 and not too much c increase? But final best by c. For direction to reduce exact, if c not immediate might not. Since objective nonconvex, can accept if phi decreases and c not worse by more than 1e-12, then maybe later. But simpler exact c descent. Use phi line to find step where phi decreases, then if c decreases keep, else maybe keep? To not get stuck, we can update if c <= c + 1e-12 and phi decrease, as long as best tracked. But may wander. Use exact c only for best; update current h if phi decreases even if c slightly worse? Could escape. But risk. Implement:
```
if c2 < c - 1e-13: accept
elif phi2 < phi - 1e-8 and c2 <= c + 1e-10: accept (if allows wander)
```
Ok.

if n>800: cap = min(cap, 300)
```
Trust radii list `[0.2,0.1,0.05,0.02,0.01,0.005]`. For each radius, multiple attempts with different top? Could order by radius decreasing; if accepted, keep same radius? But as solution changes, may still improve. Use while.

```
def lp_polish(h,n,deadline,max_rounds=200):
    best=h.copy(); best_c=compute_c5(h,n)
    h=best.copy(); c=best_c
    cap = min(2*n-1, max(50, int(0.5*n)))
    if n > 700: cap = min(cap, 250)
    radii=[0.2,0.1,0.05,0.02,0.01,0.005,0.002]
    # Also top overrides
    top_multipliers=[0.2,0.5,1.0]
    r_idx=0
    no_improve=0
    while time.time()<deadline and r_idx < len(radii):
       r_radii=radii[r_idx]
       improved_any=False
       for mult in top_multipliers:
           top=max(10,int(cap*mult))
           # try several rounds at same top/r

               res=trust_region_lp_step(h,n,deadline,top,r_radii)

                   h,c=res[1],res[2]
                   if c < best_c - 1e-13:

                   improved_any=True
                   break
           if improved_any: break? maybe continue next multiplier?
       if improved_any:
           no_improve=0
           # small chance to keep same radius another cycle? continue loop? We'll not increment radius? But avoid infinite. Could after improvement, let while continue but reset no_improve and maybe use next top? Better increment? If radius yielded improvement, continue same radius next while because cur solution changed. So don't increment on improved.
       else:
           r_idx += 1
    return best,best_c
```

8. `binary_round(h,n)` top-level:
```
def binary_round(h,n):
    target=n//2
    idx=np.argsort(h)[::-1]
    hb=np.zeros(n,dtype=float)
    hb[idx[:target]]=1.0
    return hb
```
9. `binary_local_search(h,n,deadline,max_evals?)`:
Need use random rng. Use `np.random.RandomState(seed?)` clone. Since `run` has rng. Function can accept `rng`.
```
def binary_local_search(h,n,deadline,rng,samples=500,sweeps=...):
   h=binary_round(h,n).copy()
   c=compute_c5(h,n)
   best_h=h.copy(); best_c=c
   zeros=np.where(h==0)[0]; ones=np.where(h==1)[0]
   # local improvement sampling random swaps
   evaluations=0
   sweeps=0
   while time.time()<deadline and sweeps<200:
       improved=False
       # sample size adapt: if n<=300: 800; elif n<=600:400; else 200
       for k in range(sample_count):
           if time.time()>deadline or evaluations>max_evals: break
           if len(zeros)<1 or len(ones)<1: break
           z_idx = rng.randint(len(zeros)); o_idx=rng.randint(len(ones))
           i=zeros[z_idx]; j=ones[o_idx]
           # modify
           h[i]=1; h[j]=0
           evaluations+=1
           c2=compute_c5(h,n)
           if c2 < best_c - 1e-14:
               best_c=c2; best_h=h.copy()
               # update zeros/ones arrays: i from zero to one, j one to zero. Use np.delete/np.concatenate
               # position of i in zeros z_idx; j in ones o_idx. But after previous accept, arrays updated.
               new_zeros = np.concatenate((zeros[:z_idx], zeros[z_idx+1:]))
               new_ones = np.concatenate((ones[:o_idx], ones[o_idx+1:], [i]))? Wait i becomes one, j becomes zero. So zeros loses i, gains j. ones loses j, gains i.
               zeros = np.concatenate((zeros[:z_idx], zeros[z_idx+1:], np.array([j])))
               ones = np.concatenate((ones[:o_idx], ones[o_idx+1:], np.array([i])))
               improved=True
               break
           else:
               h[i]=0; h[j]=1
       sweeps+=1
       if not improved: break
   return best_h,best_c
```

But after reject, arrays unchanged. Good.

Let's determine np.correlate order precisely.
Definition from numpy (as of 1.25): `np.correlate(a, v, mode='full')`:
```
c_k = \sum_n a_{n+k} \bar{b_n}
```

Detailed function implementation:

```
def compute_c5(h, n):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    if n <= 1: return 0.0
    val = np.max(np.correlate(h, 1.0-h, mode='full'))
    if val < 0 and val > -1e-12: val=0.0
    return float(val * (2.0/n))
```
Use `float(np.max(...))`. If n=1? Constraints sum 0.5? not used. Return 0.

`project_feasible` as discussed. Need copy bool? If h is int array, convert float. If project on duplicated with sum exact, okay.

`term_grad_counts` as above. For h length n.

`smooth_phi_grad_obj`: For n maybe >1000 skip? But used. If alpha huge, `np.exp(a)` with negative large underflows ok. Use `a = alpha*(obj-cmax); a -= a.max()` (max 0 due to cmax but rounding maybe positive? use `a = a - np.max(a)`). `sumexp = np.exp(a).sum()`; if 0? set w first? cmax ensures at least one exp(0)=1. Good. active threshold `1e-10`. If alpha high, only active max. If all weights small? okay.

Term gradient loop could be slow if active large. To cap cost, if active count > 200 and n>300, maybe use only top weights? But weights sum 1; small weights negligible. threshold 1e-8. For low alpha, weights ~1/M; if M=999, threshold 1e-8 includes all (1/999=1e-3 > threshold). So active all. To limit, set threshold = max(1e-9, 1.0/(len(obj)*50))? That would include top ~2%. `projected_smooth_polish`:
```
def projected_smooth_polish(h,n,deadline,alphas=(100,500,2000,10000),max_iter=80):
   best_h=np.array(h.copy()); best_c=compute_c5(best_h,n)
   for alpha in alphas:
      h=best_h.copy(); c=best_c
      for it in range(max_iter):
         if time.time()>deadline-1: break
         phi,g=smooth_phi_grad_obj(h,n,alpha)
         if not np.isfinite(phi) or not np.all(np.isfinite(g)): break
         ninf=np.linalg.norm(g, ord=np.inf)
         if ninf <=1e-13: break
         d=-g/ninf
         accepted=False
         for step in [0.1,0.05,0.2,0.02,0.01,0.005]:
             h2=project_feasible(h+step*d,n)
             c2=compute_c5(h2,n)
             if c2 < c - 1e-13:
                 h,c=h2,c2; accepted=True; break
             elif np.isfinite(c2):
                 # maybe accept phi decrease
                 phi2,g2=smooth_phi_grad_obj(h2,n,alpha) if False? no, expensive. Skip.
         if not accepted: break
         if c < best_c - 1e-12:
             best_c=c; best_h=h.copy()
   return best_h,best_c
```
Maybe too little. Could use exact c only.

`trust_region_lp_step` details with `linprog`:
```
def trust_region_lp_step(h,n,deadline,top_count,trust):
    vals = np.correlate(h,1.0-h)
    c_counts = float(np.max(vals))
    if not np.isfinite(c_counts): return (False,h,compute_c5(h,n),0.0)
    c_obj = c_counts*2.0/n
    order = np.argsort(vals)[::-1]
    active = order[:max(1,top_count)]
    # add near max if top_count small? Could include up to min(len, top_count*2)
    # Use np.flatnonzero(vals >= c_counts - 1e-7)
    near = np.flatnonzero(vals >= c_counts - 1e-7)
    if near.size:
       active = np.unique(np.concatenate((active,near)))
    # if too many, keep top top_count plus near max? Use top top_count
    if active.size > top_count:
       active = order[:top_count]
    m=active.size
    G = np.empty((m,n), dtype=float)
    for ii in range(m): G[ii,:]=term_grad_counts(h,n,int(active[ii]))
    cvec = np.zeros(n+1); cvec[n]=1.0
    A = np.empty((m,n+1)); b = np.empty(m)
    for ii in range(m):
       A[ii,:n]=G[ii]
       A[ii,n]=-1.0
       b[ii]=-vals[active[ii]]
    low = np.maximum(-h, -trust); high=np.minimum(1.0-h, trust)
    # Ensure low <= high
    high=np.maximum(high, low)
    bounds=[]
    for i in range(n): bounds.append((float(low[i]),float(high[i])))
    bounds.append((-1.0, None))
    Aeq=np.ones((1,n)); beq=np.array([0.0])
    try:
       res=linprog(cvec, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, A_eq=Aeq, b_eq=beq, bounds=bounds, method='highs')
    except Exception:
       return (False,h,c_obj,0.0)
    if (not res.success) or res.x is None:
       return (False,h,c_obj,0.0)
    delta=np.array(res.x[:n], dtype=float)
    # clean
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(delta)): return false
    delta -= delta.mean()
    # clip to bounds again
    delta = np.minimum(np.maximum(delta, low-h? wait bounds on delta; low/high are δ bounds), high)
    # If projection? We'll project h+step*delta.
    for step in (1.0,0.6,0.35,0.2,0.1,0.06,0.03,0.015):
        h2=project_feasible(h + step*delta, n)
        c2=compute_c5(h2,n)
        if np.isfinite(c2) and c2 < c_obj - 1e-12:
            return (True,h2,c2,step)
    return (False,h,c_obj,0.0)
```
Clipping δ: `delta=np.clip(delta, low, high)` but low/high arrays. If high<low? fixed. Need ensure after clip sum not 0; re-sub mean? If clip changes sum, project later. But sum constraint was important; after clip, line search projection will reproject sum, but δ not exactly sum 0. That's okay for candidate; project ensures feasible. Could maybe break LP direction. Fine. To respect, don't clip unless necessary; project_feasible will enforce bounds. But δ may exceed low/high if linprog tolerance; project handles. We can just `delta = np.clip(delta, -10*trust, 10*trust)`? not.

If line search fails but linear model predicts negative t, maybe no exact improvement. OK.

Potential issue: LP can choose δ that changes h far from current even if `step=1` and near bounds? project clips. Good.

`lp_polish`:
```
def lp_polish(h,n,deadline):
    best_h=np.array(h,dtype=float); best_h=project_feasible(best_h,n)
    best_c=compute_c5(best_h,n)
    h=best_h.copy(); c=best_c
    total_vals=2*n-1
    base_cap = max(20, min(total_vals, n//3))
    if n>700: base_cap=min(base_cap,200)
    caps = [max(10,int(base_cap*0.35)), base_cap, min(total_vals, int(base_cap*1.8))]
    caps = sorted(set(caps))
    radii=[0.1,0.05,0.02,0.01,0.005,0.002]
    ridx=0
    stall=0
    while time.time() < deadline - 3 and ridx < len(radii):
        r = radii[ridx]
        improved_any = False
        for cap in caps:
            if time.time() > deadline - 3: break
            for trial in range(4):
                if time.time() > deadline - 3: break
                accepted,h2,c2,_ = trust_region_lp_step(h,n,deadline-3,cap,r)
                if accepted:
                    h=h2; c=c2
                    if c < best_c - 1e-13:
                        best_c=c; best_h=h.copy()
                    improved_any=True
                    break
            if improved_any:
                break
        if improved_any:
            stall=0
            # maybe continue same r; to avoid too many same r, could not increment
            # but if it keeps accepting same cap, fine. After no improvement next loop, increment.
        else:
            stall += 1
            if stall >= 2:
                ridx += 1
    return best_h,best_c
```
But if improved_any true, it will immediately loop again with same radii and caps. It will start from cap1 and trial1, etc. It may keep improving many times; good. However if after one accept then next small improvement maybe step 1e-13; accept condition in lp step uses 1e-12, eventually no. Good. If improved any, doesn't increment radii. Could get stuck in same cap finding same direction? If direction still improves, okay. If line search fails no increment until two no-improve cycles. Good.
Need ensure `lp_polish` doesn't take forever if it keeps tiny improvements under tolerance? lp step demands c2<c_obj-1e-12, so finite.

Call `lp_polish` with deadlines. It may do many LP solves. Good.

`binary_local_search` code refine:
```
def binary_local_search(h,n,deadline,rng):
    h=binary_round(h,n).copy()
    c=compute_c5(h,n)
    best_c=c; best_h=h.copy()
    zeros=np.where(h==0.0)[0]
    ones=np.where(h==1.0)[0]
    if len(zeros)==0 or len(ones)==0: return best_h,best_c
    # sample size
    if n <= 300: sample=700; max_eval=20000
    elif n <= 600: sample=400; max_eval=8000
    else: sample=200; max_eval=4000
    evals=0; sweeps=0; no_improve_sweeps=0
    while time.time() < deadline - 3 and sweeps < 300 and evals < max_eval:
       improved=False
       for _ in range(sample):
          if time.time()>deadline-3 or evals>=max_eval: break
          i=zeros[rng.randint(zeros.size)]
          j=ones[rng.randint(ones.size)]
          # h binary, but if i/j same? impossible
          h[i]=1.0; h[j]=0.0; evals+=1
          c2=compute_c5(h,n)
          if c2 < best_c - 1e-13:
             best_c=c2; best_h=h.copy()
             # update lists
             pos_z = np.where(zeros==i)[0]
             pos_o = np.where(ones==j)[0]
             zeros = np.delete(zeros, pos_z)
             zeros = np.concatenate((zeros, np.array([j],dtype=zero?)))
             ones = np.delete(ones, pos_o)
             ones = np.concatenate((ones, np.array([i])))
             improved=True
             break
          else:
             h[i]=0.0; h[j]=1.0
       sweeps+=1
       if not improved:
          no_improve_sweeps +=1
          if no_improve_sweeps >= 2: break
       else:
          no_improve_sweeps=0
    return best_h,best_c
```
The `np.where(zeros==i)` O(n) after many accepts; could use indices from sample by maintaining positions. Since sample chooses `zi = rng.randint`; after previous accepts, arrays unordered. We need position. Could use `dict` mapping? Not worth. n small.

But after reject, arrays unchanged; after accept, we delete i from zeros and j from ones then append. However if `i` may not be exactly in zeros due to floating? It was chosen from zeros. Good. But if i or j arrays dtype int. Good.

Could use `zeros = zeros[zeros != i]` but if duplicates none. Position find for delete. Use np.delete with condition might delete all. Simpler:
```
zeros = zeros[(zeros != i)]
zeros = np.append(zeros, j)
ones = ones[(ones != j)]
ones = np.append(ones, i)
```
This avoids pos. O(n). But if multiple same? no duplicates.

Important: We set h[i]=1,h[j]=0. If i/j chosen; after accept, best_h copies current h. Good.
If reject, we reset. If i==j impossible zeros/ones sets disjoint.

Sample count and max_eval: If deadline long, max_eval might stop early, no improvement; okay. Could allow more if time. Use `max_eval = int(20000 * (budget_fraction?)` not. We can set based on deadline remaining:
```
remaining = max(0,deadline-time.time())
if n<=300: max_eval=int(min(30000, remaining*1000)) # 1000 evals/s? maybe too high
```
Simpler fixed 15000. For 250, 15k maybe 3s. For 1000, maybe 15s but sample smaller. OK.

hp = h + amp*rng.standard_normal(n)
return project_feasible(hp,n)
```

Pseudo:
```
def run(seed=42,budget_s=1100,**kwargs):
    t0 = time.time()
    deadline = t0 + float(budget_s) - 8.0
    if deadline - t0 < 30: deadline = t0 + max(5,float(budget_s)-2)
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)
    try:
        h0 = np.array(initial_h_values, dtype=float).copy()
        n0 = len(h0)
    except Exception:
        n0 = 128
        h0 = np.full(n0, 0.5)
    if n0 <= 0:
        n0=128; h0=np.full(n0,0.5)
    h0 = project_feasible(h0,n0)
    best_h = h0.copy(); best_n=n0; best_c=compute_c5(best_h,best_n)
    useful = max(60.0, float(budget_s)-10.0)
    # phase deadlines relative to t0; but if budget small adjust
    dl_lp1 = t0 + 0.32*useful
    dl_bin = t0 + 0.48*useful
    dl_binlp = t0 + 0.62*useful
    dl_smooth = t0 + 0.72*useful
    dl_up = t0 + 0.88*useful
    dl_final1 = t0 + 0.96*useful
    # phase1 LP parent
    if time.time() < dl_lp1:
       hl,cl = lp_polish(best_h,best_n,dl_lp1)
       if cl < best_c: best_h=hl.copy(); best_c=cl; best_n=best_n
    # phase2 binary local search on best_n (or 500 if?) maybe from best_h
    if time.time() < dl_bin:
       hb,cb = binary_local_search(best_h,best_n,dl_bin,rng)
       if cb < best_c:
          best_h=hb.copy(); best_c=cb; best_n=best_n
    # phase3 LP from binary
    if time.time() < dl_binlp:
       hl,cl = lp_polish(best_h,best_n,dl_binlp)
       if cl < best_c: update
    # phase4 smooth
    if time.time() < dl_smooth and best_n <= 600:
       hs,cs = projected_smooth_polish(best_h,best_n,dl_smooth)
       update
    # phase5 upscale 2 if best_n*2 <=1000
    if time.time() < dl_up and best_n*2 <= 1000:
       hh = np.repeat(best_h, 2)
       nn = best_n*2
       hh = project_feasible(hh,nn)
       # evaluate; even if not better, polish
       hl,cl = lp_polish(hh,nn,dl_up)
       if cl < best_c:
          best_h=hl.copy(); best_c=cl; best_n=nn
    # maybe if still time, upscale again from best (if <=1000)
    if time.time() < dl_final1 and best_n*2 <= 1000:
       hh = np.repeat(best_h,2)
       nn = best_n*2
       hh=project_feasible(hh,nn)
       hl,cl=lp_polish(hh,nn,dl_final1)
       if cl < best_c: update
    # phase random perturb and LP short
    if time.time() < deadline - 40:
       attempt=0
       while time.time() < deadline - 40 and attempt < 4:
          amp = 0.05*(attempt+1)
          hp = best_h + amp*rng.standard_normal(best_n)
          hp = project_feasible(hp,best_n)
          # short LP with deadline limited to t + 15? Need absolute deadline
          dls = min(deadline - 40 - attempt*5, time.time()+20)
          hl,cl=lp_polish(hp,best_n,dls)
          if cl < best_c: update
          attempt+=1
    # final LP
    if time.time() < deadline - 3:
       hl,cl=lp_polish(best_h,best_n,deadline-3)
       if cl < best_c: update
    # final projection and c
    best_h=project_feasible(best_h,best_n)
    best_c=compute_c5(best_h,best_n)
    # ensure finite; if not, fallback initial
    if not np.isfinite(best_c):
       best_h=project_feasible(h0,n0); best_c=compute_c5(best_h,n0); best_n=n0
    return best_h, float(best_c), int(best_n)
```

Maybe implement a custom "pair LP": choose a pair (zero i, one j) and optimize the amount of mass t from j to i exactly. This preserves sum exactly and captures bilinear terms exactly (quadratic in t). This can improve continuous and binary to fractional. Let's design because it's powerful and simple.

Pair transfer direction: Move amount δ from h[j] to h[i]: h[i]+=δ_t, h[j]-=δ_t, where 0≤δ_t≤min(1-h[i], h[j]). Other variables fixed. Objective as function of t: max over all lags of linear or quadratic terms. The terms involving i and j are bilinear; if both appear in same term and product h_i*h_j, as function of t, t(1-t) quadratic. We can minimize max of functions over t in interval. def pair_descent_polish(h,n,deadline,max_rounds=200):
   best... current...
   base_vals = None
   rounds=0
   while time<deadline:
      if base_vals is None:
         base_vals = np.correlate(h,1-h)
         current_c = base_vals.max()*dx
      cands = candidate_pairs(h,n,base_vals,rng,cap=400)
      # if no cands, add random high-low 200
      improved=False
      for (i,j) in cands:
         if time>deadline: break
         if h[i] <= h[j]+1e-13: continue
         cap=min(1-h[i],h[j]); if cap<=1e-13: continue
         # maybe if h[j]-h[i] large? ok
         t,ccount = best_pair_t(base_vals, grad_i, grad_j, q, cap)
         if t <=1e-14 or t >= cap-1e-14:
            # boundary maybe only return; still evaluate? best_pair_t evaluates endpoints.
         if ccount*dx < current_c - 1e-12:
            h[i]+=t; h[j]-=t
            current_c = ccount*dx
            if current_c < best_c: update
            base_vals=None
            improved=True
            break
      if not improved:
         # try 100 random pairs
         for _ in range(100): ... same
         if no improve: break
      rounds+=1
   # final recompute

Can't nested. Implement add inline loop. Need no closures. We'll write top-level helper without nested.
```
def candidate_pairs(h,n,base_vals,rng,count=400):
    cmax = base_vals.max()
    tol = max(1e-8, 0.05*cmax)  # within 5% of max? Could include many if flat. Use top 20 regardless.
    order = np.argsort(base_vals)[::-1]
    top = order[:min(25, len(order))]
    n1=n-1; pairs=[]; seen=set()
    for idx in top:
       if idx == n1: continue
       if idx < n1:
          e = n1 - idx; L = n - e
          # deterministic p's
          ps = [0, L//2, L-1]
          if L > 10: ps.extend(rng.randint(0,L, size=8).tolist())
          for p in ps:
             if p<0 or p>=L: continue
             if h[p] > h[p+e] + 1e-10:
                i=p+e; j=p
                if h[i] < h[j] - 1e-12 and (i,j) not in seen:
                    seen.add((i,j)); pairs.append((i,j))
       else:
          e = idx - n1; L = n - e
          ps = [0, L//2, L-1]
          if L>10: ps.extend(rng.randint(0,L,size=8).tolist())
          for p in ps:
             if p<0 or p>=L: continue
             if h[p+e] > h[p] + 1e-10:
                i=p; j=p+e
                if h[i] < h[j] - 1e-12 and (i,j) not in seen:
                    seen.add((i,j)); pairs.append((i,j))
    # add random high-low up to count
    if len(pairs) < count:
       idx_all = np.arange(n); low_mask = h < h.mean(); high_mask = h > h.mean()
       lows = idx_all[low_mask]; highs=idx_all[high_mask]
       if lows.size and highs.size:
          for _ in range((count-len(pairs))*4):
             i = lows[rng.randint(lows.size)]; j=highs[rng.randint(highs.size)]
             if h[i] < h[j] - 1e-12 and (i,j) not in seen:
                seen.add((i,j)); pairs.append((i,j))
                if len(pairs)>=count: break
    return pairs[:count]
```
Tol variable not used; can remove. Top 25 enough. If base_vals flat, active top arbitrary.

`best_pair_t` implementation top-level:
```
def best_pair_t(base_vals, diffg, q, cap):
    if cap <= 1e-14: return 0.0, float(base_vals.max())
    t_best=0.0; c_best=float(base_vals.max())
    K=17
    lo=0.0; hi=cap
    # First grid full interval
    for stage in range(2):
       ts=np.linspace(lo, hi, K)
       M=base_vals.size
       # To reduce memory, compute max over terms by loop over ts?
       vals = base_vals[None,:] + diffg[:,None]*ts[None,:] + q[:,None]*ts[None,:]*ts[None,:]
       cts = np.max(vals, axis=0)
       imin = int(np.argmin(cts))
       if imin == 0:
           t_best=lo; c_best=float(cts[0]); 
       elif imin == K-1:
           t_best=hi; c_best=float(cts[-1]);
       else:
           lo = ts[imin-1] if imin>0 else ts[0]
           hi = ts[imin+1] if imin<K-1 else ts[-1]
           t_best=ts[imin]; c_best=float(cts[imin])
       if hi-lo <= 1e-12*max(1.0,cap): break
    # final eval at t_best? compute exact
    ft = base_vals + diffg*t_best + q*t_best*t_best
    c_best = float(np.max(ft))
    t_best = float(t_best)
    return t_best, c_best
```
The memory `base_vals[None,:] + diffg[:,None]*ts[None,:]` creates MxK. M up to ~1999, K=17 ->34k; fine. But for two stages maybe okay. `q[:,None]*ts[None,:]**2` compute. Could be inefficient if q mostly zeros; but fine.

Need note: t_best from grid may be at boundary; final eval. If c_best not lower than base max, no improvement.

`grad_component_all_terms`:
```
def grad_component_all_terms(p,n,h):
    U=np.zeros(n-1, dtype=float); V=np.zeros(n-1, dtype=float)
    n2 = n-p-1  # number e 1..n-p-1
    if n2 > 0:
        U[:n2] += 1.0 - h[p+1:n]
        V[:n2] += -h[p+1:n]
    pcount = p  # e 1..p
    if pcount > 0:
        rev = h[p-1::-1]
        U[:pcount] += -rev
        V[:pcount] += 1.0 - rev
    full = np.empty(2*n-1, dtype=float)
    full[:n-1] = U[::-1]
    full[n-1] = 1.0 - 2.0*h[p]
    full[n:] = V
    return full
```
Check lengths: For p=0, n2=n-1, U[:n-1]+=1-h[1:n] (U first for e=1..n-1? For p=0 in U first: e < n, all e 1..n-1 yes; second none; V first none; V second e=1..n-1 all, V[:n-1]+=-h[1:n]. Good.
For p=n-1, n2=0, pcount=n-1: U[:n-1]+=-h[n-2::-1] (e=1..n-1 second: h[p-e]=h[n-1-e] from n-2 down0; good); V[:n-1]+=1-h[n-2::-1] (V first e=1..n-1; no V second? p<n-e? none; good). Good.

`make_q_full(d,n)`:
```
def make_q_full(d,n):
    M=2*n-1; n1=n-1
    q=np.zeros(M,dtype=float)
    q[n1] = -2.0
    if d != 0 and d < n:
        q[n1-d] = 1.0
        q[n1+d] = 1.0
    return q
```
If d=0 (i=j) not used; q center remains -2 but if same variable transfer cap 0.

Potential issue: For pair distance d, if both positions appear in a term with d=0? no. q center -2 correct. If d such that n1-d <0? d<n yes.

`pair_descent_polish` top-level:
```
def pair_descent_polish(h,n,deadline,rng,max_rounds=300):
    h = project_feasible(h,n); best_h=h.copy(); best_c=compute_c5(h,n); current_c=best_c
    base_counts = None
    rounds=0
    while time.time() < deadline - 2 and rounds < max_rounds:
        if base_counts is None:
            base_counts = np.correlate(h, 1.0-h, mode='full').astype(float, copy=False)
            current_c = float(base_counts.max()) * (2.0/n)
        cands = candidate_pairs(h,n,base_counts,rng,400)
        improved=False
        for i,j in cands:
            if time.time() > deadline - 2: break
            if h[i] >= h[j] - 1e-13: continue
            cap = min(1.0 - h[i], h[j])
            if cap <= 1e-13: continue
            gi = grad_component_all_terms(i,n,h)
            gj = grad_component_all_terms(j,n,h)
            diff = gi - gj
            q = make_q_full(abs(i-j),n)
            t,ccount = best_pair_t(base_counts, diff, q, cap)
            if t <= 1e-14: continue
            if ccount * (2.0/n) < current_c - 1e-12:
                h[i] += t
                h[j] -= t
                # clip small
                h[i] = min(max(h[i],0.0),1.0); h[j]=...
                current_c = ccount * (2.0/n)
                if current_c < best_c - 1e-12:
                    best_c = current_c; best_h = h.copy()
                base_counts = None
                improved=True
                break
        if not improved:
            # random pairs
            if len(cands)==0: cands = candidate_pairs... maybe random? candidate_pairs includes random.
            # try 2000 random? We can just call again; if no improvement, break
            # To avoid immediate break, maybe add random only in candidate_pairs. If no pair among 400 improves, likely local but maybe try another set
            for _ in range(10):
                if time.time() > deadline: break
                cands2 = candidate_pairs(h,n,base_counts,rng,400)
                for i,j in cands2:
                   ... if accept break
                if improved: break
            if not improved: break
        rounds += 1
    # exact recompute
    h=project_feasible(h,n)
    best_h=project_feasible(best_h,n)? if best from h maybe.
    c=compute_c5(h,n); if c<best_c... best_h=h.copy()
    return best_h,best_c
```
Need not project many times. Since pair transfer preserves sum exactly if h sum exact; clipping afterwards. But if h has tiny bounds, ok.

The repeated `candidate_pairs` uses `h.mean()` each random generation O(n) and top sort O(n log n). For each failed set, okay. But `np.argsort` on 499 values trivial. For n=1000, 1999. Good.

Performance of `best_pair_t`: In candidate loop, computing `gi`, `gj` O(n). For 400 candidates, 800 grad arrays; each alloc length M ~1000. If n=250, 800*500=400k ops; fine. But many vectors allocations. Could be okay.

Can we cache q by distance to avoid allocate per candidate? Use function with memo? No closures. Top-level list global? Cannot maintain? We can make a simple cache dict at module level? Better avoid global mutable? Use local in run? But helper cannot keep state unless global. We can just allocate; M small.

Potential issue: `best_pair_t` uses `base_counts` from before pair transfer. If candidate pair has i,j values such that cap small, exact formula. Good.

Could pair descent get stuck because it only transfers from high to low in one direction? Since we always set i lower than j, and t positive, it moves mass from higher to lower. This tends to equalize and can change objective. It cannot move mass from lower to higher, but by choosing i/j reversed (i low,j high), all directions essentially: any pair and direction corresponds to moving mass from one to the other; if desired direction is high to low? What if optimum requires moving mass from lower to higher (e.g., making a 0 into 1 and a 1 into 0 but the target zero is currently lower, so high to low captures that); moving from lower to higher would increase the target and decrease source making more extreme; but that is equivalent to choosing i (new high? no, mass source must have enough). Any transfer between two variables can be represented as moving some mass from the one with larger current value to the one with smaller if t>0 and cap limited; moving from smaller to larger would be negative t, which we don't consider because interval [0,cap] with cap from high to low only. Is negative t ever beneficial? Yes, to increase the lower and decrease the lower? Wait if current x<y, moving positive t from y to x equalizes. ```
def candidate_pairs(h,n,base_vals,rng,count=500):
    n1=n-1
    cmax = float(base_vals.max())
    # If cmax tiny? still.
    order = np.argsort(base_vals)[::-1]
    top_count = min(25, len(order))
    pairs=[]; seen=set()
    for ai in range(top_count):
       idx = int(order[ai])
       if idx == n1:
          frac = np.where((h > 0.02) & (h < 0.98))[0]
          for _ in range(20):
             if frac.size == 0: break
             a = int(frac[rng.randint(frac.size)])
             b = int(rng.randint(n))
             if a != b:
                key=(a,b)
                if key not in seen:
                   seen.add(key); pairs.append(key)
          continue
       if idx < n1:
          e = n1 - idx
          L = n - e
          # deterministic and random p
          ps = [0, L//2, L-1]
          if L > 6:
             ps.extend(int(x) for x in rng.randint(0, L, size=8))
          for p in ps:
             if 0 <= p < L:
                a = p + e; b = p
                key=(a,b)
                if a != b and key not in seen:
                   seen.add(key); pairs.append(key)
       else:
          e = idx - n1
          L = n - e
          ps = [0, L//2, L-1]
          if L > 6:
             ps.extend(int(x) for x in rng.randint(0,L,size=8))
          for p in ps:
             if 0 <= p < L:
                a = p; b = p + e
                key=(a,b)
                if key not in seen:
                   seen.add(key); pairs.append(key)
    if len(pairs) < count:
       # random ordered distinct pairs
       attempts = (count - len(pairs))*4 + 20
       for _ in range(attempts):
          a = int(rng.randint(n)); b = int(rng.randint(n))
          if a == b: continue
          key=(a,b)
          if key not in seen:
             seen.add(key); pairs.append(key)
             if len(pairs) >= count: break
    return pairs[:count]
```

```python
import time
import math
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import linprog


def compute_c5(h, n):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    if n <= 1:
        return 0.0
    val = float(np.max(np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode='full')))
    if val < 0.0 and val > -1e-12:
        val = 0.0
    return val * (2.0 / float(n))


def project_feasible(h, n):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float).copy()
    target = 0.5 * float(n)
    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)

    for _ in range(60):
        s = float(h.sum())
        diff = target - s
        if abs(diff) < 1e-13:
            break
        if diff > 0.0:
            w = 1.0 - h
            ws = float(w.sum())
            if ws < 1e-15:
                break
            h += (diff / ws) * w
        else:
            ws = float(h.sum())
            if ws < 1e-15:
                break
            h += (diff / ws) * h

    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)

    for _ in range(30):
        s = float(h.sum())
        diff = target - s
        if abs(diff) < 1e-14:
            break
        if diff > 0.0:
            idx = int(np.argmin(h))
            cap = 1.0 - h[idx]
            if cap <= 1e-15:
                w = 1.0 - h
                ws = float(w.sum())
                if ws <= 1e-15:
                    break
                h += (diff / ws) * w
            else:
                h[idx] += min(diff, cap)
        else:
            idx = int(np.argmax(h))
            cap = h[idx]
            if cap <= 1e-15:
                ws = float(h.sum())
                if ws <= 1e-15:
                    break
                h += (diff / ws) * h
            else:
                h[idx] -= min(-diff, cap)

    return np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)


def term_grad_counts(h, n, full_idx):
    g = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    n1 = n - 1
    if full_idx == n1:
        g = 1.0 - 2.0 * h
    elif full_idx < n1:
        e = n1 - full_idx
        L = n - e
        g[:L] += 1.0 - h[e:n]
        g[e:n] += -h[:L]
    else:
        e = full_idx - n1
        L = n - e
        g[e:n] += 1.0 - h[:L]
        g[:L] += -h[e:n]
    return g


def grad_component_all_terms(p, n, h):
    U = np.zeros(n - 1, dtype=float)
    V = np.zeros(n - 1, dtype=float)

    n2 = n - p - 1
    if n2 > 0:
        seg = h[p + 1:n]
        U[:n2] += 1.0 - seg
        V[:n2] += -seg

    pc = p
    if pc > 0:
        rev = h[p - 1::-1]
        U[:pc] += -rev
        V[:pc] += 1.0 - rev

    full = np.empty(2 * n - 1, dtype=float)
    full[:n - 1] = U[::-1]
    full[n - 1] = 1.0 - 2.0 * h[p]
    full[n:] = V
    return full


def make_q_full(d, n):
    M = 2 * n - 1
    n1 = n - 1
    q = np.zeros(M, dtype=float)
    q[n1] = -2.0
    if 0 < d < n:
        q[n1 - d] = 1.0
        q[n1 + d] = 1.0
    return q


def best_pair_t(base_vals, diff, q, lo, hi):
    if hi <= lo + 1e-15:
        return 0.0, float(np.max(base_vals))

    t_best = 0.0
    c_best = float(np.max(base_vals))
    width0 = max(1.0, abs(hi - lo))
    K = 25

    for _ in range(3):
        ts = np.linspace(lo, hi, K)
        ts2 = ts * ts
        arr = base_vals[:, None] + diff[:, None] * ts[None, :] + q[:, None] * ts2[None, :]
        cts = np.max(arr, axis=0)
        idx = int(np.argmin(cts))
        t_best = float(ts[idx])
        c_best = float(cts[idx])

        if idx == 0:
            new_lo = ts[0]
            new_hi = ts[1] if K > 1 else ts[-1]
        elif idx == K - 1:
            new_lo = ts[-2] if K > 1 else ts[0]
            new_hi = ts[-1]
        else:
            new_lo = ts[idx - 1]
            new_hi = ts[idx + 1]

        if new_hi - new_lo <= 1e-12 * width0:
            break
        lo = float(new_lo)
        hi = float(new_hi)

    ft = base_vals + diff * t_best + q * (t_best * t_best)
    c_best = float(np.max(ft))
    return t_best, c_best


def candidate_pairs(h, n, base_vals, rng, count=500):
    n1 = n - 1
    order = np.argsort(base_vals)[::-1]
    top_count = min(25, len(order))
    pairs = []
    seen = set()

    for ai in range(top_count):
        idx = int(order[ai])

        if idx == n1:
            frac = np.where((h > 0.02) & (h < 0.98))[0]
            for _ in range(24):
                if frac.size == 0:
                    break
                a = int(frac[rng.randint(frac.size)])
                b = int(rng.randint(n))
                if a != b:
                    key = (a, b)
                    if key not in seen:
                        seen.add(key)
                        pairs.append(key)
            continue

        if idx < n1:
            e = n1 - idx
            L = n - e
            ps = [0, L // 2, L - 1]
            if L > 6:
                rv = rng.randint(0, L, size=8)
                for val in rv:
                    ps.append(int(val))
            for p in ps:
                if 0 <= p < L:
                    a = p + e
                    b = p
                    if a != b:
                        key = (a, b)
                        if key not in seen:
                            seen.add(key)
                            pairs.append(key)
        else:
            e = idx - n1
            L = n - e
            ps = [0, L // 2, L - 1]
            if L > 6:
                rv = rng.randint(0, L, size=8)
                for val in rv:
                    ps.append(int(val))
            for p in ps:
                if 0 <= p < L:
                    a = p
                    b = p + e
                    if a != b:
                        key = (a, b)
                        if key not in seen:
                            seen.add(key)
                            pairs.append(key)

    if len(pairs) < count:
        attempts = 4 * (count - len(pairs)) + 30
        for _ in range(attempts):
            a = int(rng.randint(n))
            b = int(rng.randint(n))
            if a == b:
                continue
            key = (a, b)
            if key not in seen:
                seen.add(key)
                pairs.append(key)
                if len(pairs) >= count:
                    break

    return pairs[:count]


def pair_descent_polish(h, n, deadline, rng, pair_count=500):
    if n < 2:
        h = project_feasible(h, n)
        return h, compute_c5(h, n)

    h = project_feasible(h, n)
    best_h = h.copy()
    best_c = compute_c5(h, n)
    current_c = best_c
    dx = 2.0 / float(n)
    base_counts = None
    rounds = 0

    while time.time() < deadline - 2.0 and rounds < 3000:
        h = project_feasible(h, n)
        if base_counts is None:
            base_counts = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode='full').astype(float, copy=False)
            current_c = float(base_counts.max()) * dx

        cands = candidate_pairs(h, n, base_counts, rng, pair_count)
        improved = False

        for pair in cands:
            if time.time() > deadline - 2.0:
                break

            i = int(pair[0])
            j = int(pair[1])
            if i == j:
                continue

            x = float(h[i])
            y = float(h[j])
            lo = max(-x, y - 1.0)
            hi = min(1.0 - x, y)
            if hi <= lo + 1e-13:
                continue

            gi = grad_component_all_terms(i, n, h)
            gj = grad_component_all_terms(j, n, h)
            diff = gi - gj
            q = make_q_full(abs(i - j), n)

            t, ccount = best_pair_t(base_counts, diff, q, lo, hi)
            if abs(t) <= 1e-14:
                continue

            if ccount * dx < current_c - 1e-12:
                h2 = h.copy()
                h2[i] += t
                h2[j] -= t
                h2 = np.clip(h2, 0.0, 1.0)
                c2 = compute_c5(h2, n)
                if np.isfinite(c2) and c2 < current_c - 1e-12:
                    h = h2
                    current_c = c2
                    base_counts = None
                    if c2 < best_c - 1e-12:
                        best_c = c2
                        best_h = h.copy()
                    improved = True
                    break

        rounds += 1
        if not improved:
            break

    best_h = project_feasible(best_h, n)
    best_c = compute_c5(best_h, n)
    return best_h, best_c


def binary_round(h, n):
    target = int(n // 2)
    idx = np.argsort(np.asarray(h, dtype=float))[::-1]
    hb = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    if target > 0:
        hb[idx[:target]] = 1.0
    return hb


def binary_local_search(h, n, deadline, rng):
    if n < 4 or (n % 2 != 0):
        return project_feasible(h, n), compute_c5(h, n)

    h = binary_round(np.asarray(h, dtype=float), n).copy()
    current_c = compute_c5(h, n)
    best_c = current_c
    best_h = h.copy()

    zeros = np.where(h < 0.5)[0]
    ones = np.where(h > 0.5)[0]

    if zeros.size == 0 or ones.size == 0:
        return h, compute_c5(h, n)

    if n <= 320:
        sample_count = 700
        max_eval = 15000
    elif n <= 640:
        sample_count = 420
        max_eval = 8000
    else:
        sample_count = 220
        max_eval = 4000

    hill_eval = max_eval // 2
    evals = 0
    sweeps = 0
    no_best_sweeps = 0
    sa_enabled = False
    T = 0.02

    while time.time() < deadline - 3.0 and sweeps < 300 and evals < max_eval:
        if evals >= hill_eval:
            sa_enabled = True

        improved_best_this_sweep = False
        for _ in range(sample_count):
            if time.time() > deadline - 3.0 or evals >= max_eval:
                break
            if zeros.size == 0 or ones.size == 0:
                break

            i = int(zeros[rng.randint(zeros.size)])
            j = int(ones[rng.randint(ones.size)])
            if i == j:
                continue

            h[i] = 1.0
            h[j] = 0.0
            evals += 1
            c2 = compute_c5(h, n)

            accept = False
            if c2 < current_c - 1e-13:
                accept = True
            elif sa_enabled:
                delta = c2 - current_c
                if delta < T:
                    try:
                        p = math.exp(-delta / T)
                    except OverflowError:
                        p = 0.0
                    if math.isfinite(p) and rng.random_sample() < p:
                        accept = True

            if accept:
                current_c = c2
                if c2 < best_c - 1e-13:
                    best_c = c2
                    best_h = h.copy()
                    improved_best_this_sweep = True
                zeros = np.where(h < 0.5)[0]
                ones = np.where(h > 0.5)[0]
                T *= 0.999
            else:
                h[i] = 0.0
                h[j] = 1.0
                T *= 0.9995

        sweeps += 1
        if improved_best_this_sweep:
            no_best_sweeps = 0
        else:
            no_best_sweeps += 1
            if no_best_sweeps > 10:
                break

    best_h = project_feasible(best_h, n)
    best_c = compute_c5(best_h, n)
    return best_h, best_c


def trust_region_lp_step(h, n, top_count, trust):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    vals = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode='full').astype(float, copy=False)
    c_counts = float(vals.max())
    c_obj = c_counts * (2.0 / float(n))
    if not math.isfinite(c_counts):
        return False, h, c_obj, 0.0

    order = np.argsort(vals)[::-1]
    active = order[:max(1, int(top_count))]
    near = np.flatnonzero(vals >= c_counts - 1e-7)
    if near.size > 0:
        active = np.unique(np.concatenate((active, near)))
    if active.size > top_count:
        active = order[:top_count]

    m = int(active.size)
    G = np.empty((m, n), dtype=float)
    for ii in range(m):
        G[ii, :] = term_grad_counts(h, n, int(active[ii]))

    cvec = np.zeros(n + 1, dtype=float)
    cvec[n] = 1.0

    A = np.empty((m, n + 1), dtype=float)
    b = np.empty(m, dtype=float)
    for ii in range(m):
        A[ii, :n] = G[ii, :]
        A[ii, n] = -1.0
        b[ii] = -float(vals[int(active[ii])])

    low = np.maximum(-h, -trust)
    high = np.minimum(1.0 - h, trust)
    high = np.maximum(high, low)

    bounds = []
    for ii in range(n):
        bounds.append((float(low[ii]), float(high[ii])))
    bounds.append((-1.0, None))

    Aeq = np.ones((1, n), dtype=float)
    beq = np.array([0.0], dtype=float)

    try:
        res = linprog(cvec, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, A_eq=Aeq, b_eq=beq, bounds=bounds)
    except Exception:
        return False, h, c_obj, 0.0

    if (not res.success) or res.x is None:
        return False, h, c_obj, 0.0

    delta = np.asarray(res.x[:n], dtype=float)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(delta)):
        return False, h, c_obj, 0.0

    delta -= float(delta.mean())
    if np.linalg.norm(delta, ord=np.inf) < 1e-12:
        return False, h, c_obj, 0.0

    for step in (1.0, 0.6, 0.35, 0.2, 0.12, 0.06, 0.03, 0.015):
        h2 = project_feasible(h + step * delta, n)
        c2 = compute_c5(h2, n)
        if np.isfinite(c2) and c2 < c_obj - 1e-12:
            return True, h2, c2, step

    return False, h, c_obj, 0.0


def lp_polish(h, n, deadline):
    if n < 2:
        h = project_feasible(h, n)
        return h, compute_c5(h, n)

    h = project_feasible(h, n)
    best_h = h.copy()
    best_c = compute_c5(h, n)

    total_vals = 2 * n - 1
    base_cap = min(total_vals, max(20, n // 3))
    if n > 700:
        base_cap = min(base_cap, 200)

    caps = [
        max(8, int(base_cap * 0.35)),
        int(base_cap),
        min(total_vals, int(base_cap * 1.8)),
    ]
    caps = sorted(set([c for c in caps if c > 0]))
    if not caps:
        caps = [max(8, total_vals)]

    radii = [0.10, 0.05, 0.025, 0.012, 0.006, 0.003]
    ridx = 0
    no_improve_rounds = 0

    while time.time() < deadline - 4.0 and ridx < len(radii):
        r = radii[ridx]
        improved_any = False

        for cap in caps:
            if time.time() > deadline - 4.0:
                break
            for _ in range(3):
                if time.time() > deadline - 4.0:
                    break
                accepted, h2, c2, _ = trust_region_lp_step(h, n, cap, r)
                if accepted:
                    h = h2
                    if c2 < best_c - 1e-12:
                        best_c = c2
                        best_h = h.copy()
                    improved_any = True
                    break
            if improved_any:
                break

        if improved_any:
            no_improve_rounds = 0
        else:
            no_improve_rounds += 1
            if no_improve_rounds >= 2:
                ridx += 1

    best_h = project_feasible(best_h, n)
    best_c = compute_c5(best_h, n)
    return best_h, best_c


def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.time()
    budget_s = float(budget_s)
    deadline = t0 + budget_s - 8.0
    if deadline <= t0 + 5.0:
        deadline = t0 + max(2.0, budget_s - 2.0)

    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)

    try:
        h0 = np.asarray(initial_h_values, dtype=float).copy()
        n0 = int(len(h0))
    except Exception:
        n0 = 250
        h0 = np.full(n0, 0.5, dtype=float)

    if n0 < 2:
        n0 = 128
        h0 = np.full(n0, 0.5, dtype=float)

    h0 = project_feasible(h0, n0)
    best_h = h0.copy()
    best_n = n0
    best_c = compute_c5(best_h, best_n)

    useful = max(60.0, budget_s - 12.0)

    dl_pair = t0 + 0.40 * useful
    dl_bin = t0 + 0.55 * useful
    dl_lp = t0 + 0.75 * useful
    dl_up = t0 + 0.90 * useful
    dl_final = deadline

    if time.time() < dl_pair - 5.0:
        hl, cl = pair_descent_polish(best_h, best_n, dl_pair, rng, 600)
        if cl < best_c - 1e-12:
            best_h = hl.copy()
            best_c = cl
            best_n = best_n

    if (best_n % 2 == 0) and time.time() < dl_bin - 5.0:
        hb, cb = binary_local_search(best_h, best_n, dl_bin, rng)
        if cb < best_c - 1e-12:
            best_h = hb.copy()
            best_c = cb
            best_n = best_n

    if time.time() < dl_lp - 5.0:
        hl, cl = lp_polish(best_h, best_n, dl_lp)
        if cl < best_c - 1e-12:
            best_h = hl.copy()
            best_c = cl
            best_n = best_n

    if best_n * 2 <= 1000 and time.time() < dl_up - 25.0:
        hh = np.repeat(best_h, 2)
        nn = int(best_n * 2)
        hh = project_feasible(hh, nn)
        if nn <= 700:
            hl, cl = pair_descent_polish(hh, nn, dl_up, rng, 450)
        else:
            hl, cl = lp_polish(hh, nn, dl_up)
        if cl < best_c - 1e-12:
            best_h = hl.copy()
            best_c = cl
            best_n = nn

    if best_n * 2 <= 1000 and time.time() < dl_final - 80.0:
        hh = np.repeat(best_h, 2)
        nn = int(best_n * 2)
        hh = project_feasible(hh, nn)
        if nn <= 700:
            hl, cl = lp_polish(hh, nn, dl_final - 80.0)
        else:
            hl, cl = lp_polish(hh, nn, min(deadline - 60.0, time.time() + 90.0))
        if cl < best_c - 1e-12:
            best_h = hl.copy()
            best_c = cl
            best_n = nn

    perturb_deadline = deadline - 60.0
    for k in range(3):
        if time.time() > perturb_deadline:
            break
        amp = 0.03 * (k + 1)
        hp = best_h + amp * rng.standard_normal(best_n)
        hp = project_feasible(hp, best_n)
        local_dl = min(perturb_deadline, time.time() + 35.0)
        if best_n <= 700:
            hl, cl = pair_descent_polish(hp, best_n, local_dl, rng, 350)
        else:
            hl, cl = lp_polish(hp, best_n, local_dl)
        if cl < best_c - 1e-12:
            best_h = hl.copy()
            best_c = cl
            best_n = best_n

    if time.time() < dl_final - 6.0:
        hl, cl = lp_polish(best_h, best_n, dl_final - 6.0)
        if cl < best_c - 1e-12:
            best_h = hl.copy()
            best_c = cl
            best_n = best_n

    if time.time() < dl_final - 2.0 and best_n <= 800:
        hl, cl = pair_descent_polish(best_h, best_n, dl_final - 2.0, rng, 300)
        if cl < best_c - 1e-12:
            best_h = hl.copy()
            best_c = cl
            best_n = best_n

    best_h = project_feasible(best_h, best_n)
    best_c = compute_c5(best_h, best_n)

    if not math.isfinite(best_c) or not np.all(np.isfinite(best_h)):
        best_h = project_feasible(h0, n0)
        best_n = n0
        best_c = compute_c5(best_h, best_n)

    return best_h, float(best_c), int(best_n)
```
